   167	
   168	def _embedding_gather(token_embed: jax.Array, token_ids: Int[Array, "B S"]) -> Float[Array, "B S D"]:
   169	    """Look up tokens from a replicated table without a cross-rack collective."""
   170	
   171	    def _local(table: jax.Array, ids: jax.Array) -> jax.Array:
   172	        return table[ids]
   173	
   174	    token_ids = reshard(token_ids, P(_BATCH_AXES, None))
   175	    return shard_map(
   176	        _local,
   177	        mesh=get_abstract_mesh(),
   178	        in_specs=(P(None, None), P(_BATCH_AXES, None)),
   179	        out_specs=P(_BATCH_AXES, None, None),
   180	    )(token_embed, token_ids)
   200	
   201	class QbEstimator(StrEnum):
   202	    """How the QB router estimates each expert's threshold beta (the (1-K/E) upper quantile of the
   203	    logit margins ``score - alpha``).
   204	
   205	    ``TOPK`` takes a per-device ``top_k`` of the margins and ``pmean``s the result -- one small
   206	    collective per layer. ``HIST`` bins the margins into ``qb_hist_bins`` over the live global
   207	    ``[min, max]`` (a ``pmin``/``pmax`` this step) and reads the quantile from the summed histogram --
   208	    a smoother estimate at the cost of a per-expert count reduction.
   209	    """
   210	
   211	    TOPK = "topk"
   212	    HIST = "hist"
   213	
   214	
   215	@dataclass(frozen=True)
   216	class GrugModelConfig:
   217	    """Hyperparameters for the grug MoE transformer.
   218	
   219	    GatedNorm and QB routing are structural. All layers are MoE.
   220	    """
   221	
   222	    vocab_size: int
   223	    hidden_dim: int = 512
   224	    intermediate_dim: int = 256
   225	    shared_expert_intermediate_dim: int = 512
   226	    num_shared_experts: int = 1
   227	    num_experts: int = 256
   228	    num_experts_per_token: int = 4
   229	    # LatentMoE (arXiv 2601.18089); latent RMSNorm per issue #6822.
   230	    latent_dim: int | None = None
   231	    qb_estimator: QbEstimator = QbEstimator.TOPK
   232	    qb_hist_bins: int = 1000  # bins for the QB histogram estimator; ignored by the top-k estimator
   233	    num_layers: int = 6
   234	    num_heads: int = 4
   235	    num_kv_heads: int = 1
   236	    local_kv_heads: int | None = None
   237	    global_kv_heads: int | None = None
   238	    head_dim: int | None = None
   239	    max_seq_len: int = 8192
   240	    sliding_window: int = 2048
   241	    global_every: int = 4
   242	    capacity_factor: float = 1.0
   243	    layer_norm_eps: float = 1e-5
   244	    initializer_std: float = 0.02
   245	    qk_mult: float = 1.3
   246	    sconv: bool = False
   247	    sconv_kernel: int = 4
   248	    sconv_sites: tuple[str, ...] = ("k", "attn", "mlp")
   249	    attention_implementation: GrugAttentionImplementation | None = None
   250	    moe_implementation: MoeImplementation | None = None
   251	    expert_chunks: int = 1
   252	    pooled_transport_capacity_factor: float | None = None
   253	    num_expert_waves: int = 1
   254	    report_capacity_overflow: bool = False
   255	    remat_mode: RematMode = "recompute_all"
   256	    """Per-block gradient checkpointing. "recompute_all" reruns the whole block in
   257	    backward (lowest memory); "save_moe" keeps the tagged MoE dispatch tensors so
   258	    backward skips re-running expert dispatch and its EP collectives; "offload_carry"
   259	    recomputes everything but stages the layer-input residual on pinned host, which
   260	    releases its HBM between forward and backward."""
   261	    remat_save_names: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   262	    """Named intermediates the remat policy keeps on device for the backward, deleting
   263	    their recompute. Names must come from REMAT_SAVEABLE_NAMES; the empty default keeps
   264	    every remat mode's existing policy unchanged. This extends only which named tensors
   265	    are saved -- the offload_carry offload list and the XLA memory-budget flags are
   266	    untouched. Saved values are bit-identical to their recompute; only residency changes."""
   267	    rope: RotaryConfig = dataclasses.field(default_factory=RotaryConfig)
   268	    rope_fused: bool = False
   269	
   270	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   271	        _ = self.inferred_head_dim
   272	        if self.num_heads % self.stored_kv_heads != 0:
   273	            raise ValueError("num_heads must be divisible by the stored KV-head count")
   274	        if (self.local_kv_heads is None) != (self.global_kv_heads is None):
   275	            raise ValueError("local_kv_heads and global_kv_heads must be set together")
   276	        if self.local_kv_heads is not None and self.global_kv_heads is not None:
   277	            if self.local_kv_heads <= 0 or self.global_kv_heads <= 0:
   278	                raise ValueError("local_kv_heads and global_kv_heads must be positive")
   279	            if self.num_heads % self.local_kv_heads != 0 or self.num_heads % self.global_kv_heads != 0:
   280	                raise ValueError("num_heads must be divisible by both local and global KV-head counts")
   281	            if self.num_kv_heads != max(self.local_kv_heads, self.global_kv_heads):
   282	                raise ValueError("num_kv_heads must equal the stored maximum of local/global KV heads")
   283	        if self.global_every <= 0:
   284	            raise ValueError("global_every must be positive")
   285	        if self.vocab_size <= 0:
   286	            raise ValueError("vocab_size must be positive")
   287	        if self.max_seq_len <= 0:
   288	            raise ValueError("max_seq_len must be positive")
   289	        if self.num_experts <= 0:
   290	            raise ValueError("num_experts must be positive")
   291	        if self.num_experts_per_token <= 0:
   292	            raise ValueError("num_experts_per_token must be positive")
   293	        if self.num_experts_per_token >= self.num_experts:
   294	            # QB routing takes top-(k+1) and keeps the last entry as the threshold alpha, so a
   295	            # full-bank top-k asks `jax.lax.top_k` for more entries than the router has experts.
   296	            raise ValueError("num_experts_per_token must be < num_experts, because QB routing selects top-(k+1)")
   297	        if self.latent_dim is not None and not 0 < self.latent_dim <= self.hidden_dim:
   298	            raise ValueError(
   299	                f"latent_dim must be in (0, hidden_dim={self.hidden_dim}], got {self.latent_dim}; "
   300	                "it only reduces communication when it is smaller than the hidden dimension"
   301	            )
   302	        if self.shared_expert_intermediate_dim < 0:
   303	            raise ValueError("shared_expert_intermediate_dim must be non-negative")
   304	        if self.capacity_factor <= 0:
   305	            raise ValueError("capacity_factor must be positive")
   306	        if self.expert_chunks <= 0:
   307	            raise ValueError("expert_chunks must be positive")
   308	        if self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor is not None and self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor <= 0:
   309	            raise ValueError("pooled_transport_capacity_factor must be positive")
   310	        if self.moe_implementation == "fixed_pooled_wave_all_to_all":
   311	            if self.pooled_transport_capacity_factor is None:
   312	                raise ValueError("fixed_pooled_wave_all_to_all requires pooled_transport_capacity_factor")
   313	        if self.num_expert_waves <= 0:
   314	            raise ValueError("num_expert_waves must be positive")
   315	        if self.num_shared_experts <= 0:
   316	            raise ValueError("num_shared_experts must be positive")
   317	        unknown_save_names = set(self.remat_save_names) - REMAT_SAVEABLE_NAMES
   318	        if unknown_save_names:
   319	            raise ValueError(
   320	                f"remat_save_names contains names with no call site: {sorted(unknown_save_names)}; "
   321	                f"choose from {sorted(REMAT_SAVEABLE_NAMES)}"
   322	            )
   323	        resolve_moe_implementation(self.moe_implementation)
   324	
   325	    @property
   326	    def Embed(self) -> Axis:
   327	        return Axis("embed", self.hidden_dim)
   328	
   329	    @property
   330	    def model_type(self) -> type["Transformer"]:
   331	        return Transformer
   332	
   333	    @property
   334	    def inferred_head_dim(self) -> int:
   335	        if self.head_dim is not None:
   336	            return self.head_dim
   337	        if self.hidden_dim % self.num_heads != 0:
   338	            raise ValueError(
   339	                f"hidden_dim={self.hidden_dim} is not divisible by num_heads={self.num_heads}; set head_dim explicitly"
   340	            )
   430	            "latent_dim": self.latent_dim,
   431	            "qk_mult": self.qk_mult,
   432	            "local_kv_heads": self.local_kv_heads,
   433	            "global_kv_heads": self.global_kv_heads,
   434	            "global_every": self.global_every,
   435	            "rope_fused": self.rope_fused,
   436	            "sconv": self.sconv,
   437	            "sconv_kernel": self.sconv_kernel,
   438	            "sconv_sites": list(self.sconv_sites),
   439	            "grugmoe_attention_mode": "production",
   440	            GRUG_MOE_ARTIFACT_SCHEMA_VERSION_KEY: GRUG_MOE_ARTIFACT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
   441	        }
   442	        if config_overrides is not None:
   443	            config.update(config_overrides)
   444	        return GrugMoeHfConfig(**config)
   445	
   446	
   447	def rms_norm(x: jax.Array, eps: float = 1e-6) -> jax.Array:
   448	    """Non-parametric RMS norm over the last dimension."""
   449	    # Name the variance, not the rsqrt: the rsqrt vjp reads its *input*, so saving the scale
   450	    # would leave the full-width square-mean reduce in the backward recompute.
   451	    variance = tree_checkpoint_name(
   452	        jnp.mean(jnp.square(x.astype(jnp.float32)), axis=-1, keepdims=True), QK_NORM_VARIANCE_REMAT_NAME
   453	    )
   454	    return (x * jax.lax.rsqrt(variance + eps)).astype(x.dtype)
   455	
   456	
   457	def _apply_rotary_embedding_fused(
   458	    q: Float[Array, "B S H D"],
   459	    k: Float[Array, "B S H D"],
   460	    *,
   461	    seq_len: int,
   462	    head_dim: int,
   463	    rotary_dim: int,
   464	    rope: RotaryConfig,
   465	    disable_rope: jax.Array | bool,
   466	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S H D"], Float[Array, "B S H D"]]:
   467	    # Rotates adjacent (interleaved) dimension pairs (i, i+1) within the rotary half -- a valid RoPE
   468	    # convention, but NOT bit-equivalent to the non-fused ``apply_rotary_embedding`` path, which pairs
   469	    # split halves (i, i + rotary_dim/2). The hero always uses this fused path; the two are not
   470	    # interchangeable mid-training (a from-scratch run learns whichever convention it starts with).
   471	    half = rotary_dim // 2
   472	    inv_freq = 1.0 / (rope.theta ** (jnp.arange(0, half, dtype=jnp.float32) / half))
   473	    angles = jnp.arange(seq_len, dtype=jnp.float32)[:, None] * inv_freq[None, :]
   474	    cos = jnp.cos(angles)
   475	    sin = jnp.sin(angles)
   476	    first_factor = jnp.repeat(cos, 2, axis=-1)
   477	    second_factor = jnp.reshape(jnp.stack([-sin, sin], axis=-1), (seq_len, rotary_dim))
   478	    if rotary_dim < head_dim:
   479	        padding = head_dim - rotary_dim
   480	        first_factor = jnp.concatenate(
   481	            [first_factor, jnp.ones((seq_len, padding), first_factor.dtype)],
   482	            axis=-1,
   483	        )
   484	        second_factor = jnp.concatenate(
   485	            [second_factor, jnp.zeros((seq_len, padding), second_factor.dtype)],
   486	            axis=-1,
   487	        )
   488	    first_factor = jnp.where(disable_rope, 1.0, first_factor)[None, :, None, :]
   489	    second_factor = jnp.where(disable_rope, 0.0, second_factor)[None, :, None, :]
   490	
   491	    def _apply(x: Float[Array, "B S H D"]) -> Float[Array, "B S H D"]:
   492	        dtype = x.dtype
   493	        flipped = jnp.flip(x.reshape(*x.shape[:-1], head_dim // 2, 2), axis=-1).reshape(x.shape)
   494	        return (first_factor * x + second_factor * flipped).astype(dtype)
   495	
   496	    return _apply(q), _apply(k)
   497	
   498	
   499	class ShortConv(eqx.Module):
   500	    """Depthwise causal 1-D convolution over the sequence axis (Inkling-style SConv).
   501	
   502	    A kernel of ``W`` taps mixes each channel with its own ``W-1`` causal predecessors,
   503	    ``out[t] = sum_{lag} weight[lag] * x[t-lag]``, independently per channel. Identity-init
   504	    (``weight[0]=1``, later taps 0) makes it a pass-through at step 0. Weights are tiny (``W*C``) and
   505	    routed to Adam. Shard-local -- no cross-channel or cross-shard dependency, so no collectives.
   506	
   507	    The body dispatches to ``levanter.kernels.pallas.short_conv``, which selects a fused Pallas
   508	    kernel on GPU and the pad-and-shift weighted sum everywhere else; see that module's docstring.
   509	    """
   510	
   511	    weight: Float[Array, "W C"]
   512	    kernel_size: int = eqx.field(static=True)
   513	
   514	    @staticmethod
   515	    def init(channels: int, kernel_size: int) -> "ShortConv":
   516	        weight = jnp.zeros((kernel_size, channels)).at[0].set(1.0)
   517	        # FSDP-shard the channel dim so the grad reduce-scatters instead of all-reducing; the
   518	        # forward gathers the weight back to replicated.
   519	        return ShortConv(weight=reshard(weight, P(None, _FSDP_AXES)), kernel_size=kernel_size)
   520	
   521	    def __call__(self, x: Float[Array, "B S C"], segment_ids: Int[Array, "B S"] | None = None) -> Float[Array, "B S C"]:
   522	        # With segment_ids (packed documents), a tap that reaches into a previous document is
   523	        # zeroed so the conv never mixes across a boundary; the lag-0 (current-token) tap is
   524	        # always kept.
   525	        weight = reshard(self.weight, P(None, None))
   526	        return short_conv(weight, x, segment_ids, batch_axes=_BATCH_AXES)
   527	
   528	
   529	class CausalSelfAttention(eqx.Module):
   530	    w_q: Float[Array, "D NH"]
   531	    w_k: Float[Array, "D MH"]
   532	    w_v: Float[Array, "D MH"]
   533	    w_o: Float[Array, "NH D"]
   534	    attn_gate: Float[Array, "D N"]
   535	    sconv_k: "ShortConv | None"  # SConv after the K projection (cfg.sconv)
   536	    cfg: GrugModelConfig = eqx.field(static=True)
   537	
   538	    @staticmethod
   539	    def init(cfg: GrugModelConfig, *, key: PRNGKeyArray) -> "CausalSelfAttention":
   540	        k_q, k_k, k_v, k_o = random.split(key, 4)
   541	        d, n, m, h = cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.num_heads, cfg.stored_kv_heads, cfg.inferred_head_dim
   542	        return CausalSelfAttention(
   543	            w_q=reshard(_init_weight(k_q, (d, n * h), cfg.initializer_std), P(_FSDP_AXES, "model")),
   544	            w_k=reshard(_init_weight(k_k, (d, m * h), cfg.initializer_std), P(_FSDP_AXES, "model")),
   545	            w_v=reshard(_init_weight(k_v, (d, m * h), cfg.initializer_std), P(_FSDP_AXES, "model")),
   546	            w_o=reshard(_init_weight(k_o, (n * h, d), cfg.initializer_std), P("model", _FSDP_AXES)),
   547	            attn_gate=reshard(jnp.zeros((d, n)), P(None, None)),
   548	            sconv_k=(ShortConv.init(m * h, cfg.sconv_kernel) if cfg.sconv and "k" in cfg.sconv_sites else None),
   549	            cfg=cfg,
   550	        )
   551	
   552	    @named_call
   553	    def __call__(
   554	        self,
   555	        x: Float[Array, "B S D"],
   556	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array,
   557	        disable_rope: bool | jax.Array = False,
   558	        is_global: bool | jax.Array = False,
   559	    ) -> Float[Array, "B S D"]:
   560	        head_dim = self.cfg.inferred_head_dim
   561	        seq_len = x.shape[1]
   562	        batch_spec = _batch_spec()
   563	
   564	        q_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_q)
   565	        k_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_k)
   566	        v_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_v)
   567	        # SConv: depthwise causal conv after the K projection. segment_ids (packed-document
   568	        # boundaries) come from the mask so the conv never mixes across a document boundary.
   569	        _seg = mask.segment_ids if isinstance(mask, AttentionMask) else None
   570	        sconv_segment_ids = _seg[0] if _seg is not None else None
   571	        if self.sconv_k is not None:
   572	            k_flat = self.sconv_k(k_flat, sconv_segment_ids)
   573	        q = rearrange(q_flat, "... (n d) -> ... n d", d=head_dim)
   574	        k = rearrange(k_flat, "... (m d) -> ... m d", d=head_dim)
   575	        v = rearrange(v_flat, "... (m d) -> ... m d", d=head_dim)
   576	
   577	        if self.cfg.local_kv_heads is not None and self.cfg.global_kv_heads is not None:
   578	            stored_kv_heads = self.cfg.stored_kv_heads
   579	
   580	            # Both branches must agree on sharding, not just shape: `lax.cond` compares full types
   581	            # under explicit mesh axes. The pass-through case keeps the projection's `model`-sharded
   582	            # head axis, while the align case slices to one head -- which cannot stay sharded -- and
   583	            # broadcasts back, giving an identical shape with a different sharding. Reshard both to
   584	            # the same spec. This is a no-op wherever the mesh leaves `model` at one, which is why
   585	            # the hero shape never hit it despite also setting local_kv_heads != global_kv_heads.
   586	            #
   587	            # Replicate the head axis rather than pinning it to `model`: a shape can carry fewer
   588	            # KV heads than the model axis is wide (d768 stores one), and the KV tensors are small
   589	            # enough -- at most a dozen heads of 128 -- that replication is not worth a special case.
   590	            kv_spec = P(_BATCH_AXES, None, None, None)
   591	
   592	            def _logical_kv(projection: jax.Array, num_kv_heads: int) -> jax.Array:
   593	                # Replicate before slicing, not after: narrowing a `model`-sharded head axis to a
   594	                # count that does not divide the mesh axis is unsupported.
   595	                projection = reshard(projection, kv_spec)
   596	                if num_kv_heads == stored_kv_heads:
   597	                    return projection
   598	                return align_kv_heads(projection[:, :, :num_kv_heads, :], num_q_heads=stored_kv_heads)
   599	
   600	            k, v = jax.lax.cond(
   601	                jnp.asarray(is_global, dtype=jnp.bool_),
   602	                lambda kv: (
   603	                    _logical_kv(kv[0], self.cfg.global_kv_heads),
   604	                    _logical_kv(kv[1], self.cfg.global_kv_heads),
   605	                ),
   606	                lambda kv: (
   607	                    _logical_kv(kv[0], self.cfg.local_kv_heads),
   608	                    _logical_kv(kv[1], self.cfg.local_kv_heads),
   609	                ),
   610	                (k, v),
   611	            )
   612	
   613	        q = rms_norm(q)
   614	        k = rms_norm(k)
   615	
   616	        # Half-RoPE: apply rotary embedding only to the first half of Q/K head_dim (second half is
   617	        # rope-free on every layer). ``disable_rope`` skips RoPE entirely on this layer -- long/global
   618	        # layers run rope-free. It rides in as a traced per-layer scalar from the layer scan, so RoPE
   619	        # is always computed and selected with ``jnp.where`` rather than a per-layer ``lax.cond``.
   620	        if self.cfg.rope_fused:
   621	            q, k = _apply_rotary_embedding_fused(
   622	                q,
   623	                k,
   624	                seq_len=seq_len,
   625	                head_dim=head_dim,
   626	                rotary_dim=head_dim // 2,
   627	                rope=self.cfg.rope,
   628	                disable_rope=disable_rope,
   629	            )
   630	        else:
   631	
   632	            def _rope(qh: jax.Array, kh: jax.Array) -> tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array]:
   633	                half = head_dim // 2
   634	                q_rot, k_rot = apply_rotary_embedding(
   635	                    qh[..., :half], kh[..., :half], seq_len=seq_len, head_dim=half, rope=self.cfg.rope
   636	                )
   637	                return (
   638	                    jnp.concatenate([q_rot, qh[..., half:]], axis=-1),
   639	                    jnp.concatenate([k_rot, kh[..., half:]], axis=-1),
   640	                )
   641	
   642	            if isinstance(disable_rope, bool):
   643	                if not disable_rope:
   644	                    q, k = _rope(q, k)
   645	            else:
   646	                q_roped, k_roped = _rope(q, k)
   647	                keep = ~jnp.asarray(disable_rope, dtype=jnp.bool_)
   648	                q = jnp.where(keep, q_roped, q)
   649	                k = jnp.where(keep, k_roped, k)
   650	        q = q * self.cfg.qk_mult
   651	        attn_out = attention(q, k, v, mask, implementation=self.cfg.attention_implementation)
   652	        # Exclusive Self Attention (XSA): subtract the component of yᵢ parallel to vᵢ, per head.
   653	        # zᵢ = yᵢ - (yᵢᵀvᵢ / ‖vᵢ‖²) vᵢ.
   654	        aligned_v = align_kv_heads(v, num_q_heads=attn_out.shape[2])
   655	        # GPU XSA with GQA can give attn_out a backend-specific head sharding;
   656	        # match v to that dynamic sharding before the per-head projection math.
   657	        aligned_v = reshard(aligned_v, _partition_spec_of(attn_out) or P(_BATCH_AXES, None, None, "model"))
   658	        dot = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.sum(attn_out * aligned_v, axis=-1, keepdims=True), XSA_MOMENT_REMAT_NAME)
   659	        v_norm_sq = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.sum(aligned_v * aligned_v, axis=-1, keepdims=True), XSA_MOMENT_REMAT_NAME)
   660	        attn_out = attn_out - (dot / (v_norm_sq + 1e-6)) * aligned_v
   661	        # Headwise gating: sigmoid(x @ attn_gate) produces one scalar per head.
   662	        gate = 2 * jax.nn.sigmoid(jnp.einsum("bsd,dn->bsn", x, self.attn_gate))[..., None]
   663	        attn_out = gate * attn_out
   664	        # Merge heads into hidden dim while keeping model-axis sharding for w_o.
   665	        attn_out = jnp.reshape(
   666	            attn_out,
   667	            (*attn_out.shape[:-2], attn_out.shape[-2] * attn_out.shape[-1]),
   668	            out_sharding=P(_BATCH_AXES, None, "model"),
   669	        )
   670	        return jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", attn_out, self.w_o, out_sharding=batch_spec)
   671	
   672	
   673	class RMSNorm(eqx.Module):
   674	    weight: jax.Array
   675	    eps: float = eqx.field(static=True)
   676	
   677	    @staticmethod
   678	    def init(dim: int, eps: float) -> "RMSNorm":
   679	        return RMSNorm(weight=jnp.ones((dim,), dtype=jnp.float32), eps=eps)
   680	
   681	    @named_call
   682	    def __call__(self, x: Float[Array, "... D"]) -> Float[Array, "... D"]:
   683	        weight = unshard(self.weight)
   684	        dtype = x.dtype
   685	        x = x.astype(jnp.float32)
   686	        # Name the variance, not the rsqrt: the rsqrt vjp reads its *input*, so saving the
   687	        # scale would leave the full-width square-mean reduce in the backward recompute.
   688	        variance = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.mean(jnp.square(x), axis=-1, keepdims=True), RMS_NORM_VARIANCE_REMAT_NAME)
   689	        normed = x * jax.lax.rsqrt(variance + self.eps)
   690	        return (normed * weight).astype(dtype)
   691	
   692	
   693	class GatedNorm(eqx.Module):
   694	    """Learnable per-dimension gating. Compensates for AdamH's bounded activation norms.
   695	    See https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22966v1"""
   696	
   697	    w_down: jax.Array
   698	    w_up: jax.Array
   699	
   700	    @staticmethod
   701	    def init(hidden_dim: int, initializer_std: float, *, key: PRNGKeyArray) -> "GatedNorm":
   702	        k_down, k_up = random.split(key)
   703	        return GatedNorm(
   704	            w_down=reshard(_init_weight(k_down, (hidden_dim, _GATED_NORM_RANK), initializer_std), P(None, None)),
   705	            w_up=reshard(_init_weight(k_up, (_GATED_NORM_RANK, hidden_dim), initializer_std), P(None, None)),
   706	        )
   707	
   708	    @named_call
   709	    def __call__(self, x: Float[Array, "... D"]) -> Float[Array, "... D"]:
   710	        gate_hidden = jnp.einsum("...d,dr->...r", x, self.w_down)
  1020	            ),
  1021	            latent_norm=None if latent is None else RMSNorm.init(latent, cfg.layer_norm_eps),
  1022	            w_latent_up=(
  1023	                None
  1024	                if latent is None
  1025	                else reshard(_init_weight(k_up, (latent, d), cfg.initializer_std), P("model", _FSDP_AXES))
  1026	            ),
  1027	            expert_mlp=MoEExpertMlp.init(
  1028	                num_experts=cfg.num_experts,
  1029	                hidden_dim=expert_width,
  1030	                intermediate_dim=cfg.intermediate_dim,
  1031	                initializer_std=cfg.initializer_std,
  1032	                key=k_expert,
  1033	                implementation=cfg.moe_implementation,
  1034	                activation=ActivationFunctionEnum.silu,
  1035	                capacity_factor=cfg.capacity_factor,
  1036	                pooled_transport_capacity_factor=cfg.pooled_transport_capacity_factor,
  1037	                expert_chunks=cfg.expert_chunks,
  1038	                num_expert_waves=cfg.num_expert_waves,
  1039	            ),
  1040	            cfg=cfg,
  1041	        )
  1042	
  1043	    @named_call
  1044	    def __call__(
  1045	        self,
  1046	        x: Float[Array, "B S D"],
  1047	    ) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S D"], dict[str, jax.Array]]:
  1048	        b, s, _ = x.shape
  1049	        x_flat = rearrange(x, "b s d -> (b s) d")
  1050	        # Keep the router path in fp32 before top-k, softmax, and QB statistics.
  1051	        router_logits = jnp.einsum("td,de->te", x_flat, reshard(self.router, P(None, None))).astype(jnp.float32)
  1052	        biased_logits = router_logits + jax.lax.stop_gradient(self.router_bias)
  1053	        router_probs = jax.nn.softmax(router_logits, axis=-1)
  1054	        # Select top-(K+1) on biased logits; the (K+1)-th is the QB threshold alpha.
  1055	        _topk_logits, selected_experts = jax.lax.top_k(biased_logits, self.cfg.num_experts_per_token + 1)
  1056	        qb_alpha = _topk_logits[:, -1:]
  1057	        selected_experts = tree_checkpoint_name(selected_experts[:, :-1], ROUTER_SELECTED_EXPERTS_REMAT_NAME)
  1058	        # Sigmoid combine weights on unbiased logits for selected experts.
  1059	        unbiased_topk = jnp.take_along_axis(router_logits, selected_experts, axis=-1)
  1060	        combine_weights_f = jax.nn.sigmoid(unbiased_topk)
  1061	        # Renormalize K combine weights to sum to ``_ROUTING_RENORM_SUM`` (baked in).
  1062	        denom = jnp.sum(combine_weights_f, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
  1063	        combine_weights_f = combine_weights_f * (_ROUTING_RENORM_SUM / (denom + 1e-9))
  1064	        combine_weights = tree_checkpoint_name(combine_weights_f.astype(x.dtype), ROUTER_COMBINE_WEIGHTS_REMAT_NAME)
  1065	        mesh = get_abstract_mesh()
  1066	        # Per-shard partials only; the cross-device reduction happens once after the layer scan.
  1067	        router_stats = _local_routing_stats(
  1068	            reshard(selected_experts, P(_BATCH_AXES, None)),
  1069	            reshard(router_probs, P(_BATCH_AXES, None)),
  1070	            reshard(router_logits, P(_BATCH_AXES, None)),
  1071	            mesh,
  1072	            num_experts=self.cfg.num_experts,
  1073	        )
  1074	        # Sharded QB: estimate each expert's threshold beta from the margins `s - alpha`.
  1075	        s_minus_alpha = reshard(router_logits - qb_alpha, P(_BATCH_AXES, None))
  1076	        if self.cfg.qb_estimator == QbEstimator.HIST:
  1077	            beta, margin_min, margin_max = _qb_beta_hist(
  1078	                s_minus_alpha,
  1079	                mesh,
  1080	                num_experts_per_token=self.cfg.num_experts_per_token,
  1190	            rms_attn=RMSNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.layer_norm_eps),
  1191	            attn_gated_norm=GatedNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.initializer_std, key=gn_attn_key),
  1192	            attn=CausalSelfAttention.init(cfg, key=attn_key),
  1193	            rms_mlp=RMSNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.layer_norm_eps),
  1194	            mlp_gated_norm=GatedNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.initializer_std, key=gn_mlp_key),
  1195	            mlp=MoEMLP.init(cfg, key=mlp_key),
  1196	            shared=shared,
  1197	            sconv_attn=(
  1198	                ShortConv.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.sconv_kernel) if cfg.sconv and "attn" in cfg.sconv_sites else None
  1199	            ),
  1200	            sconv_mlp=(
  1201	                ShortConv.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.sconv_kernel) if cfg.sconv and "mlp" in cfg.sconv_sites else None
  1202	            ),
  1203	        )
  1204	
  1205	    @named_call
  1206	    def __call__(
  1207	        self,
  1208	        x: Float[Array, "B S D"],
  1209	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array,
  1210	        disable_rope: bool | jax.Array = False,
  1211	        is_global: bool | jax.Array = False,
  1212	    ) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S D"], dict[str, jax.Array]]:
  1213	        # A remat policy acts on named intermediates, and a block argument is not one. This
  1214	        # reassignment routes every use below through the name, which lets the policy offload it.
  1215	        x = tree_checkpoint_name(x, LAYER_CARRY_REMAT_NAME)
  1216	        # segment_ids (packed-document boundaries) for the branch-output SConvs; None when unpacked.
  1217	        _seg = mask.segment_ids if isinstance(mask, AttentionMask) else None
  1218	        sconv_segment_ids = _seg[0] if _seg is not None else None
  1219	
  1220	        attn_in = self.attn_gated_norm(self.rms_attn(x))
  1221	        attn_out = self.attn(attn_in, mask, disable_rope=disable_rope, is_global=is_global)
  1222	        if self.sconv_attn is not None:
  1223	            attn_out = self.sconv_attn(attn_out, sconv_segment_ids)
  1224	        x = x + attn_out
  1225	        mlp_in = self.mlp_gated_norm(self.rms_mlp(x))
  1226	        mlp_out, router_stats = self.mlp(mlp_in)
  1227	        if self.shared is not None:
  1228	            for shared_expert in self.shared:
  1229	                mlp_out = mlp_out + shared_expert(mlp_in, activation=ActivationFunctionEnum.silu)
  1230	        if self.sconv_mlp is not None:
  1231	            mlp_out = self.sconv_mlp(mlp_out, sconv_segment_ids)
  1232	        x = x + mlp_out
  1233	        return x, router_stats
  1234	
  1235	
  1236	def _long_layer_schedule(num_layers: int, global_every: int) -> jax.Array:
  1237	    # Every global_every-th layer is full-causal, and the last layer always is, so a depth that is
  1238	    # not a multiple of global_every still ends on a global-context layer.
  1239	    layer_indices = jnp.arange(num_layers)
  1240	    return (((layer_indices + 1) % global_every) == 0) | (layer_indices == num_layers - 1)
  1241	
  1242	
  1243	class Transformer(eqx.Module):
  1244	    token_embed: jax.Array
  1245	    embed_norm: RMSNorm
  1246	    embed_gated_norm: GatedNorm
  1247	    output_proj: jax.Array
  1248	    stacked_blocks: ArrayStacked[Block]
  1249	    final_norm: RMSNorm
  1250	    final_gated_norm: GatedNorm
  1251	    config: GrugModelConfig = eqx.field(static=True)
  1252	
  1253	    @staticmethod
  1254	    def init(
  1255	        cfg_or_vocab: GrugModelConfig | Axis,
  1256	        config: GrugModelConfig | None = None,
  1257	        *,
  1258	        key: PRNGKeyArray,
  1259	    ) -> "Transformer":
  1260	        if isinstance(cfg_or_vocab, Axis):
  1261	            if config is None:
  1262	                raise ValueError("config must be provided when initializing with a Vocab axis")
  1263	            cfg = (
  1264	                config
  1265	                if cfg_or_vocab.size == config.vocab_size
  1266	                else dataclasses.replace(config, vocab_size=cfg_or_vocab.size)
  1267	            )
  1268	        else:
  1269	            if config is not None:
  1270	                raise ValueError("config must not be provided when initializing directly from GrugModelConfig")
  1271	            cfg = cfg_or_vocab
  1272	
  1273	        embed_key, out_key, embed_gn_key, final_gn_key, *block_keys = random.split(key, cfg.num_layers + 4)
  1274	        token_embed = reshard(
  1275	            _init_weight(embed_key, (cfg.vocab_size, cfg.hidden_dim), cfg.initializer_std), _EMBED_PARTITION_SPEC
  1276	        )
  1277	        output_proj = reshard(
  1278	            _init_weight(out_key, (cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.vocab_size), cfg.initializer_std), _LM_HEAD_PARTITION_SPEC
  1279	        )
  1280	        stacked_blocks = ArrayStacked.init(cfg.num_layers, Block)(cfg, key=jnp.stack(block_keys))
  1281	        return Transformer(
  1282	            token_embed=token_embed,
  1283	            embed_norm=RMSNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.layer_norm_eps),
  1284	            embed_gated_norm=GatedNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.initializer_std, key=embed_gn_key),
  1285	            output_proj=output_proj,
  1286	            stacked_blocks=stacked_blocks,
  1287	            final_norm=RMSNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.layer_norm_eps),
  1288	            final_gated_norm=GatedNorm.init(cfg.hidden_dim, cfg.initializer_std, key=final_gn_key),
  1289	            config=cfg,
  1290	        )
  1291	
  1292	    @property
  1293	    def Vocab(self) -> Axis:
  1294	        return Axis("vocab", self.config.vocab_size)
  1295	
  1296	    @named_call
  1297	    def __call__(
  1298	        self,
  1299	        token_ids: Int[Array, "B S"],
  1300	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array | None = None,
  1301	    ) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S D"], dict[str, jax.Array]]:
  1302	        if mask is None:
  1303	            mask = AttentionMask.causal()
  1304	
  1305	        cfg = self.config
  1306	        hidden = _embedding_gather(self.token_embed, token_ids)
  1307	        hidden = self.embed_gated_norm(self.embed_norm(hidden))
  1308	
  1309	        # Local layers use a sliding window; every global_every-th layer is full causal.
  1310	        segment_ids = None
  1311	        if isinstance(mask, AttentionMask) and mask.segment_ids is not None:
  1312	            # Pin the per-token [B, S] segment ids batch-sharded before they enter the layer-scan
  1313	            # and use one array for both sides of self-attention. Resharding the original tuple
  1314	            # separately loses object identity and adds a runtime equality conditional whose output
  1315	            # is pinned to device 0.
  1316	            q_segment_ids, _ = mask.segment_ids
  1317	            q_segment_ids = _batch_reshard(q_segment_ids)
  1318	            segment_ids = (q_segment_ids, q_segment_ids)
  1319	        short_mask = AttentionMask(is_causal=True, sliding_window=cfg.sliding_window, segment_ids=segment_ids)
  1320	        long_mask = AttentionMask(is_causal=True, sliding_window=None, segment_ids=segment_ids)
  1321	
  1322	        # `remat_save_names` extends every mode with device-resident saves of the named
  1323	        # per-token tensors; the empty default reproduces each mode's existing policy exactly.
  1324	        extra_save_names = list(cfg.remat_save_names)
  1325	        if cfg.remat_mode == "save_moe":
  1326	            remat_policy = jax.checkpoint_policies.save_only_these_names(*MOE_REMAT_SAVE_NAMES, *extra_save_names)
  1327	        elif cfg.remat_mode == "offload_carry":
  1328	            # XLA stacks each offloaded name's scan residuals into one pinned allocation. Adding
  1329	            # names is therefore not free: carry plus the attention residuals asked for 72.56 GiB
  1330	            # per rank, above the node's host budget.
  1331	            remat_policy = jax.checkpoint_policies.save_and_offload_only_these_names(
  1332	                names_which_can_be_saved=extra_save_names,
  1333	                names_which_can_be_offloaded=[LAYER_CARRY_REMAT_NAME],
  1334	                offload_src="device",
  1335	                offload_dst="pinned_host",
  1336	            )
  1337	        elif extra_save_names:
  1338	            remat_policy = jax.checkpoint_policies.save_only_these_names(*extra_save_names)
  1339	        else:
  1340	            remat_policy = None
  1341	
  1342	        # Homogeneous scan: one compiled Block body over the stacked layers. The per-layer
  1343	        # short/long choice rides in as a Bool[num_layers] scan input.
  1344	        mask_schedule = _long_layer_schedule(cfg.num_layers, cfg.global_every)
  1345	        # Precompute the FA4 per-token metadata for both the full-causal (long) and sliding-window
  1346	        # (short) layers outside the scan, then select per layer with a jnp.where inside the body:
  1347	        # the sliding window is a static AttentionMask field the scan body cannot vary, so the
  1348	        # per-layer window rides in as the selected bound arrays. ``valid`` is window-independent, so
  1349	        # it is shared; long/global layers run rope-free (``disable_rope`` is the per-layer scalar).
  1350	        batch_size, seq_len = hidden.shape[0], hidden.shape[1]
  1351	        long_lower_bounds, valid = fa4_cute_segment_bounds(
  1352	            long_mask, batch_size=batch_size, seq_len=seq_len, sliding_window=None
  1353	        )
  1354	        short_lower_bounds, _ = fa4_cute_segment_bounds(
  1355	            short_mask, batch_size=batch_size, seq_len=seq_len, sliding_window=cfg.sliding_window
  1356	        )
  1357	        long_lower_bounds = _batch_reshard(long_lower_bounds)
  1358	        short_lower_bounds = _batch_reshard(short_lower_bounds)
  1359	        valid = _batch_reshard(valid)
  1360	
  1361	        def _scan_layers(
  1362	            carry_hidden: Float[Array, "B S D"],
  1363	            scan_inputs: tuple[Block, jax.Array],
  1364	        ) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S D"], dict[str, jax.Array]]:
  1365	            layer, layer_use_long_mask = scan_inputs
  1366	            use_long = jnp.asarray(layer_use_long_mask, dtype=jnp.bool_)
  1367	            lower_bounds = jnp.where(use_long, long_lower_bounds, short_lower_bounds)
  1368	            layer_mask = long_mask.with_fa4_bounds(lower_bounds, valid)
  1369	            return eqx.filter_checkpoint(layer, policy=remat_policy)(
  1370	                carry_hidden,
  1371	                layer_mask,
  1372	                use_long,
  1373	                use_long,
  1374	            )
  1375	
  1376	        hidden, stacked_router_stats = jax.lax.scan(
  1377	            _scan_layers, hidden, xs=(self.stacked_blocks.stacked, mask_schedule)
  1378	        )
  1379	        # One cross-device reduction for the whole stack of layers, instead of one inside every
  1380	        # scan iteration. See `_local_routing_stats`.
  1381	        reduced_router_stats = _reduce_router_stats(
  1382	            stacked_router_stats,
  1383	            num_experts=cfg.num_experts,
  1384	            num_experts_per_token=cfg.num_experts_per_token,
  1385	            num_tokens=batch_size * seq_len,
  1386	        )
  1387	        router_metrics = {
  1388	            "routing_entropy_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["routing_entropy"],
  1389	            "routing_counts_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["routing_counts"],
  1390	            "load_balancing_loss_per_layer": reduced_router_stats["load_balancing_loss"],
   180	        # the output. It accumulates in fp32 like the einsum below and keeps the routing
   181	        # weight in fp32 through the multiply, where the einsum has to cast it down to avoid
   182	        # promoting the larger operand, so the two agree to a single rounding.
   183	        return sonic_gather_sum(intermediate, positions.reshape(tokens_per_shard, topk), combine_weights_local)
   184	    unsorted = _sort_activations(intermediate, positions)
   185	    reshaped = unsorted.reshape(tokens_per_shard, topk, -1)
   186	    return jnp.einsum(
   187	        "tkd,tk->td", reshaped, combine_weights_local.astype(reshaped.dtype), preferred_element_type=jnp.float32
   188	    )
   189	
   190	
   191	@functools.partial(jax.custom_vjp, nondiff_argnums=(2,))
   192	def _gather_dispatch_rows(
   193	    x_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal H"], sorted_indices: Int[Array, "TK"], topk: int
   194	) -> Float[Array, "TK H"]:
   195	    """Build the expert-sorted dispatch buffer with one gather.
   196	
   197	    Equivalent to ``jnp.repeat(x_local, topk, axis=0)[sorted_indices]`` without
   198	    materializing the repeated buffer or running a data-sized permute. The backward pass
   199	    is the transpose: each token sums the cotangent rows of its ``topk`` sorted slots.
   200	    """
   201	    return x_local[sorted_indices // topk]
   202	
   203	
   204	def _gather_dispatch_rows_fwd(
   205	    x_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal H"], sorted_indices: Int[Array, "TK"], topk: int
   206	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "TK H"], Int[Array, "TK"]]:
   207	    return _gather_dispatch_rows(x_local, sorted_indices, topk), sorted_indices
   208	
   209	
   210	def _gather_dispatch_rows_bwd(
   211	    topk: int, sorted_indices: Int[Array, "TK"], cotangent: Float[Array, "TK H"]
   212	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "Tlocal H"], None]:
   213	    tokens_per_shard = sorted_indices.shape[0] // topk
   214	    positions = jnp.argsort(sorted_indices).reshape(tokens_per_shard, topk)
   215	    if sonic_gather_sum_available():
   216	        ones = jnp.ones((tokens_per_shard, topk), dtype=jnp.float32)
   217	        grad_x = sonic_gather_sum(cotangent, positions, ones)
   218	    else:
   219	        grad_x = jnp.sum(cotangent[positions], axis=1, dtype=jnp.float32)
   220	    return grad_x.astype(cotangent.dtype), None
   221	
   222	
   223	_gather_dispatch_rows.defvjp(_gather_dispatch_rows_fwd, _gather_dispatch_rows_bwd)
   224	
   225	
   226	def _loop_local_zeros(
   227	    rows: int, hidden_dim: int, dtype, tie: Int[Array, "..."], site: int = 0
   228	) -> Float[Array, "rows H"]:
   229	    """Zero buffer for an in-place ``ragged_all_to_all`` output slot, built so XLA never copies it.
   230	
   231	    ``ragged_all_to_all`` writes its result in place into the output-init operand. A literal
   232	    ``jnp.zeros`` init is a trace-time constant: it gets hoisted out of the layer scan (and
   233	    identical inits CSE together), so CopyInsertion must mint a fresh multi-GB device copy of
   234	    the pristine zeros into the a2a's output slot on every scan iteration. Deriving the zeros
   235	    from ``tie`` -- a per-site, per-iteration traced value -- keeps each init inside the loop
   236	    body and distinct from every other init, so it lowers to one write-only fill kernel whose
   237	    buffer the a2a updates directly.
   238	
   239	    ``tie`` must be a non-negative integer array (group or transfer sizes): with ``site >= 0``,
   240	    ``min(tie[0], -site) + site`` is exactly zero at runtime but not foldable at compile time.
   241	
   242	    ``site`` must be distinct per call site. Two sites can hold the SAME tie tensor without it
   243	    being obvious (dispatch send_sizes and return recv_sizes are one tensor in ep_common), and
   244	    same-shape same-tie fills CSE into one value with two destructive in-place a2a consumers --
   245	    which makes CopyInsertion mint back the multi-GB copy this helper exists to prevent.
   246	    """
   247	    zero = (jnp.minimum(tie.reshape(-1)[0], -site) + site).astype(dtype)
   248	    return jax.lax.broadcast(zero, (rows, hidden_dim))
   249	
   250	
   251	@functools.partial(jax.custom_vjp, nondiff_argnums=(0, 1))
   252	def _dispatch_ragged_a2a(
   253	    capacity: int,
   254	    source_rows: int,
   255	    chunk_source: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   256	    input_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   257	    send_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   258	    output_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   259	    recv_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   260	) -> Float[Array, "C H"]:
   261	    """Dispatch-direction ``ragged_all_to_all`` into a freshly zeroed receiver buffer.
   262	
   263	    Owning the output-init here (instead of taking a caller-built ``jnp.zeros``) lets both the
   264	    primal and the transposed direction build their inits with `_loop_local_zeros`. jax's
   265	    built-in transpose rule inits the swapped a2a with plain zeros, which XLA hoists out of
   266	    the layer loop and re-copies every iteration; the custom vjp below is that same rule with
   267	    the init construction replaced. Zeros stay load-bearing: receiver rows no peer writes must
   268	    read 0 downstream, and dropped source rows must read a 0 cotangent in the backward.
   269	    """
   270	    del source_rows
   271	    output_init = _loop_local_zeros(capacity, chunk_source.shape[1], chunk_source.dtype, send_sizes, site=1)
   272	    return jax.lax.ragged_all_to_all(
   273	        chunk_source, output_init, input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes, axis_name="expert"
   274	    )
   275	
   276	
   277	def _dispatch_ragged_a2a_fwd(
   278	    capacity: int,
   279	    source_rows: int,
   280	    chunk_source: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   281	    input_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   282	    send_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   283	    output_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   284	    recv_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   285	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "C H"], tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array]]:
   286	    result = _dispatch_ragged_a2a(
   287	        capacity, source_rows, chunk_source, input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes
   288	    )
   289	    return result, (input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes)
   290	
   291	
   292	def _dispatch_ragged_a2a_bwd(
   293	    capacity: int,
   294	    source_rows: int,
   295	    residuals: tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array],
   296	    cotangent: Float[Array, "C H"],
   297	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "TK H"], None, None, None, None]:
   298	    del capacity
   299	    input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes = residuals
   300	    # The transpose of a ragged all-to-all is the reverse ragged all-to-all: swap the send and
   301	    # recv roles and exchange the offset vectors across shards, exactly as jax's transpose rule
   302	    # does. Source rows that were clipped receive no cotangent and keep the init's zeros.
   303	    output_offsets_x = jax.lax.all_to_all(output_offsets, "expert", 0, 0, tiled=True)
   304	    input_offsets_x = jax.lax.all_to_all(input_offsets, "expert", 0, 0, tiled=True)
   305	    # Tie to this side's send_sizes, not recv_sizes: the return path's passthrough fill ties to
   306	    # its own send_sizes, which is the SAME tensor as this dispatch's recv_sizes, and two fills
   307	    # with identical shape and tie CSE back into one value with a destructive consumer --
   308	    # re-minting exactly the copy this construction removes.
   309	    init = _loop_local_zeros(source_rows, cotangent.shape[1], cotangent.dtype, send_sizes, site=2)
   310	    source_ct = jax.lax.ragged_all_to_all(
   311	        cotangent, init, output_offsets_x, recv_sizes, input_offsets_x, send_sizes, axis_name="expert"
   312	    )
   313	    return source_ct, None, None, None, None
   314	
   315	
   316	_dispatch_ragged_a2a.defvjp(_dispatch_ragged_a2a_fwd, _dispatch_ragged_a2a_bwd)
   317	
   318	
   319	@functools.partial(jax.custom_vjp, nondiff_argnums=(0,))
   320	def _return_ragged_a2a(
   321	    capacity: int,
   322	    out_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
   323	    returned: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   324	    input_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   325	    send_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   326	    output_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   327	    recv_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   328	) -> Float[Array, "TK H"]:
   329	    """Return-direction ``ragged_all_to_all``: write expert outputs back over ``returned``.
   330	
   331	    Same construction as `_dispatch_ragged_a2a`: the primal is unchanged, and the custom vjp
   332	    replicates jax's transpose rule operation for operation, replacing only the transposed
   333	    a2a's zero init so it is built per-iteration instead of hoisted and copied.
   334	    """
   335	    del capacity
   336	    return jax.lax.ragged_all_to_all(
   337	        out_dispatch, returned, input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes, axis_name="expert"
   338	    )
   339	
   340	
   341	def _return_ragged_a2a_fwd(
   342	    capacity: int,
   343	    out_dispatch: Float[Array, "C H"],
   344	    returned: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   345	    input_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   346	    send_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   347	    output_offsets: Int[Array, "U"],
   348	    recv_sizes: Int[Array, "U"],
   349	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "TK H"], tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array]]:
   350	    result = _return_ragged_a2a(
   351	        capacity, out_dispatch, returned, input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes
   352	    )
   353	    return result, (input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes)
   354	
   355	
   356	def _return_ragged_a2a_bwd(
   357	    capacity: int,
   358	    residuals: tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array, jax.Array],
   359	    cotangent: Float[Array, "TK H"],
   360	) -> tuple[Float[Array, "C H"], Float[Array, "TK H"], None, None, None, None]:
   361	    input_offsets, send_sizes, output_offsets, recv_sizes = residuals
   362	    output_offsets_x = jax.lax.all_to_all(output_offsets, "expert", 0, 0, tiled=True)
   363	    input_offsets_x = jax.lax.all_to_all(input_offsets, "expert", 0, 0, tiled=True)
   364	    init = _loop_local_zeros(capacity, cotangent.shape[1], cotangent.dtype, recv_sizes, site=3)
   365	    out_dispatch_ct = jax.lax.ragged_all_to_all(
   366	        cotangent, init, output_offsets_x, recv_sizes, input_offsets_x, send_sizes, axis_name="expert"
   367	    )
   368	    # Rows this a2a overwrote took their value from ``out_dispatch``, so the pass-through
   369	    # cotangent to ``returned`` is zeroed on exactly the written intervals. This mirrors jax's
   370	    # transpose rule op for op (interval marks, cumsum, integer select_n) so the behavior is
   371	    # bit-identical, including on degenerate empty-group offset patterns.
   372	    interval_marks = (
   373	        jnp.zeros(cotangent.shape[0], jnp.int32).at[output_offsets_x].set(1).at[output_offsets_x + recv_sizes].add(-1)
   374	    )
   375	    written = jnp.broadcast_to(jnp.cumsum(interval_marks)[:, None], cotangent.shape)
   376	    passthrough_zero = _loop_local_zeros(cotangent.shape[0], cotangent.shape[1], cotangent.dtype, send_sizes, site=4)
   377	    returned_ct = jax.lax.select_n(written, cotangent, passthrough_zero)
   378	    return out_dispatch_ct, returned_ct, None, None, None, None
   379	
   380	
   381	_return_ragged_a2a.defvjp(_return_ragged_a2a_fwd, _return_ragged_a2a_bwd)
   382	
   383	
   384	def _moe_mlp_ep_ragged_a2a_local(
   385	    x_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal H"],
   386	    selected_experts_local: Int[Array, "Tlocal K"],
   387	    combine_weights_local: Float[Array, "Tlocal K"],
   388	    moe_w13_local: Float[Array, "Elocal H I2"],
   389	    moe_w2_local: Float[Array, "Elocal I H"],
   390	    *,
   395	    local_experts = moe_w13_local.shape[0]
   396	    if num_experts % local_experts != 0:
   397	        raise ValueError(
   398	            f"num_experts={num_experts} must be divisible by local expert count={local_experts} in EP mode"
   399	        )
   400	
   401	    shard_id = jax.lax.axis_index("expert")
   402	    ep_size = num_experts // local_experts
   403	    tokens_per_shard = x_local.shape[0]
   404	    topk = selected_experts_local.shape[1]
   405	    assignments_per_shard = tokens_per_shard * topk
   406	    local_capacity = int(math.ceil(capacity_factor * assignments_per_shard))
   407	    local_capacity = max(local_experts, local_capacity)
   408	
   409	    # Local experts are processed in sequential chunks so only one chunk's transport buffers
   410	    # are live at a time. The a2a outputs cannot be rematerialized (XLA never recomputes
   411	    # collectives), so unchunked they pin [capacity, H] + [TK, H] per block window and the
   412	    # hero step no longer fits next to NCCL's pools. Capacity splits evenly across chunks,
   413	    # which also makes drop clipping per-chunk.
   414	    chunks = _EXPERT_CHUNKS if local_experts % _EXPERT_CHUNKS == 0 and _EXPERT_CHUNKS > 1 else 1
   415	    chunk_experts = local_experts // chunks
   416	    chunk_capacity = max(chunk_experts, int(math.ceil(local_capacity / chunks)))
   417	    hidden_dim = x_local.shape[1]
   418	
   419	    with jax.named_scope("dispatch"):
   420	        flat_selected = selected_experts_local.reshape(-1)  # [TK]
   421	        sorted_indices = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.argsort(flat_selected), RAGGED_SORTED_INDICES_REMAT_NAME)  # [TK]
   422	        group_sizes = tree_checkpoint_name(
   423	            jnp.bincount(flat_selected, length=num_experts).astype(jnp.int32), RAGGED_GROUP_SIZES_REMAT_NAME
   424	        )  # [E]
   425	        sorted_x = _gather_dispatch_rows(x_local, sorted_indices, topk)  # [TK, H]
   426	        all_group_sizes = jax.lax.all_gather(group_sizes, "expert")  # [S, E]
   427	
   428	    expert_mlp = _select_expert_mlp(activation_fn)
   429	    chunk_of_expert = (jnp.arange(num_experts, dtype=jnp.int32) % local_experts) // chunk_experts  # [E]
   430	    # Built per-iteration (not jnp.zeros) so the layer scan does not re-copy a hoisted
   431	    # constant into the in-place return a2a's output slot every step; see _loop_local_zeros.
   432	    # The zeros are load-bearing: rows no peer writes back must read 0 in the combine.
   433	    returned = _loop_local_zeros(assignments_per_shard, hidden_dim, x_local.dtype, group_sizes, site=5)  # [TK, H]
   434	    accepted_local = jnp.zeros((), dtype=jnp.int32)
   435	    for chunk_index in range(chunks):
   436	        with jax.named_scope(f"moe_chunk_{chunk_index}"):
   437	            chunk_all_group_sizes = jnp.where(chunk_of_expert[None, :] == chunk_index, all_group_sizes, 0)  # [S, E]
   438	            clipped_group_sizes = _clip_receiver_group_sizes(
   439	                chunk_all_group_sizes,
   440	                local_expert_size=local_experts,
   441	                receiver_capacity=chunk_capacity,
   442	            )
   443	            # Sender starts come from the full (unmasked) sizes, so each chunk reads its
   444	            # groups' accepted prefixes in place in the shared sorted buffer.
   445	            dispatch_params, return_params = _expert_granular_a2a_params(
   446	                all_group_sizes,
   447	                clipped_group_sizes,
   448	                shard_id,
   449	                local_expert_size=local_experts,
   450	            )
   451	            # Serialize the chunks. Without this barrier, the scheduler can start the dispatch
   452	            # of every chunk at the same time. This causes the high memory use that the chunks
   453	            # prevent. A variant that overlaps one transport with the MLP stays within memory.
   454	            # But it does not increase the speed. The transport and the MLP compete for the
   455	            # same SMs.
   456	            chunk_source, _ = jax.lax.optimization_barrier((sorted_x, returned))
   457	            # Accepted rows are the prefix of each unclipped expert group and receiver offsets
   458	            # pack arrivals expert-major, so the received buffer feeds the grouped MLP
   459	            # directly: no sender compaction and no receiver-side permute.
   460	            x_dispatch = _dispatch_ragged_a2a(  # [C, H]
   461	                chunk_capacity,
   462	                assignments_per_shard,
   463	                chunk_source,
   464	                *dispatch_params,
   465	            )
   466	            active_all = jnp.sum(  # [Elocal]
   467	                clipped_group_sizes.reshape(ep_size, ep_size, local_experts)[:, shard_id, :], axis=0
   468	            )
   469	            active_group_sizes = active_all[
   470	                chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts
   471	            ]  # [Echunk]
   472	            total_valid = jnp.sum(active_group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32)
   473	            physical_group_sizes = active_group_sizes.at[-1].add(chunk_capacity - total_valid)  # [Echunk]
   474	            out_dispatch = expert_mlp(  # [C, H]
   475	                x_dispatch,
   476	                moe_w13_local[chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts],
   477	                moe_w2_local[chunk_index * chunk_experts : (chunk_index + 1) * chunk_experts],
   478	                physical_group_sizes,
   479	                active_group_sizes,
   480	                activation_fn,
   481	            )
   482	            # The mirror of dispatch: valid prefixes land back at unclipped sorted positions.
   483	            # Chaining every chunk through one output buffer composes the disjoint writes;
   484	            # dropped rows keep the buffer's zeros, so the final gather-sum reads dropped
   485	            # slots as zero contributions with no expansion step.
   486	            returned = _return_ragged_a2a(
   487	                chunk_capacity,
   488	                out_dispatch,
   489	                returned,
   490	                *return_params,
   491	            )
   492	            accepted_local = accepted_local + jnp.sum(clipped_group_sizes[shard_id], dtype=jnp.int32)
   493	
   494	    with jax.named_scope("combine"):
   495	        out_local = _unpermute_from_global_expert(
   496	            returned,
   497	            sorted_indices,
   498	            combine_weights_local,
   499	            tokens_per_shard=tokens_per_shard,
   500	            topk=topk,
   501	        ).astype(x_local.dtype)
   502	        dropped_local = jnp.sum(group_sizes, dtype=jnp.int32) - accepted_local
   503	        dropped_total = jax.lax.psum(dropped_local, _batch_axes(jax.sharding.get_abstract_mesh()))
   504	    return out_local, CapacityOverflow(sender=dropped_total, receiver=jnp.zeros_like(dropped_total))
   720

def tree_checkpoint_name(x: T, name: str) -> T:
    """
    Checkpoint a tree of arrays with a given name. This is useful for gradient checkpointing.
    This is equivalent to calling [jax.ad_checkpoint.checkpoint_name][]
    except that it works for any PyTree, not just arrays.

    See Also:
        * [jax.ad_checkpoint.checkpoint_name][]
        * [haliax.nn.ScanCheckpointPolicy][]
    """

    def _checkpoint_leaf(x):
        if is_jax_array_like(x):
            return checkpoint_name(x, name)
        else:
            return x

    return jax.tree.map(_checkpoint_leaf, x)

def checkpoint_name(x, name):
  """Identifies a value with a name within :func:`jax.checkpoint`.

  This function acts as an identity function at runtime (returning ``x``
  unchanged) but attaches a string name to the value in the JAX trace.
  These names can be targeted by specific checkpointing policies (see
  :ref:`checkpoint-policies`) to control which intermediate values
  are saved during the forward pass and which are recomputed during the
  backward pass.

  Args:
    x: array or PyTree of arrays to be named.
    name: A string name to associate with the value ``x``.

  Returns:
    The input ``x``, unchanged.

  See Also:
    - :func:`jax.checkpoint` (alias: :func:`jax.remat`): decorator to
      enable checkpointing.
    - :mod:`jax.checkpoint_policies`: a namespace containing policies
      that use names marked via ``checkpoint_name`` to determine behavior.

  Example:
    >>> import jax
    >>> import jax.numpy as jnp
    >>> from jax.ad_checkpoint import checkpoint_name

    >>> # Define a function where we explicitly name an intermediate value
    >>> def f(x):
    ...   y = jnp.sin(x)
    ...   z = checkpoint_name(y, "my_intermediate")
    ...   return jnp.cos(z)

    >>> # Use a policy that saves only the named value
    >>> policy = jax.checkpoint_policies.save_only_these_names("my_intermediate")
    >>> f_checkpointed = jax.checkpoint(f, policy=policy)

    For further examples, see the `remat example notebook
    <https://docs.jax.dev/en/latest/notebooks/autodiff_remat.html>`_.
  """
  if config.remat3.value:
    return tree_map(lambda x: checkpoint_name3(name, x), x)
  return tree_map(partial(name_p.bind, name=name), x)

    59	
    60	def with_transport_remat_mode(model: GrugModelConfig) -> GrugModelConfig:
    61	    """Give the ragged transport the layer-carry offload that its scheduling posture depends on.
    62	
    63	    Staging the carry on pinned host lowered the measured HBM peak by 36 GiB, which is what makes
    64	    the latency-hiding scheduler fit. The other transports are unmeasured with the offload, which
    65	    is the only reason they do not take it.
    66	
    67	    The scheduler reads the remat mode, so a launcher that skips this keeps the scheduling
    68	    posture it was measured under, exactly as it did before the mode existed.
    69	    """
    70	    if model.moe_implementation != RAGGED_MOE_IMPLEMENTATION:
    71	        return model
    72	    return dataclasses.replace(model, remat_mode="offload_carry")
    73	
    74	
    75	class HeroThroughputResult(Artifact):
    76	    """Metrics artifact for an EP hero run."""
    77	
    78	
    79	def hero_grug_trainer_config(
    80	    *,

    83	    watch_mode: WatchMode,

    86	    opt_state_resident_expert_leaves: int = 0,
    87	) -> GrugTrainerConfig:
    88	    """Set the Grug options that affect the compiled hero step."""
    89	    return GrugTrainerConfig(
    90	        data_seed=None,
    70	    ) -> GrugMoeMuonHConfig:
    71	        """MuonH optimizer with LR / beta2 / epsilon scaled to this token budget (1pct-noclip schedule)."""
    72	        tokens_per_batch = batch_size * seq_len

    77	            min_lr_ratio=self.min_lr_ratio,
    78	            lr_schedule=self.lr_schedule,
    79	            beta1=self.beta1,
    80	            beta2=self._beta2(tokens_per_batch),
    81	            epsilon=self._epsilon(tokens_per_batch, tokens),
    82	        )

   107	    # The wide 128x64 forward tile gains 0.28 MFU at the hero's 24k-restore operating point
   108	    # (mfu24-ctrl-a5/b 23.06/23.05 vs mfu24-tile-a/b 23.32/23.37, same-night interleaved).
   109	    attention_implementation="gpu_fa4_cute_wide",
   110	    moe_implementation="ragged_all_to_all",
   111	    pooled_transport_capacity_factor=1.15,  # pooled-wave only
   112	    num_expert_waves=3,  # pooled-wave only
   113	    expert_chunks=1,
   114	    report_capacity_overflow=True,
   115	    rope_fused=True,
   120	    # run stops at (a restore resumes mid-schedule, so it must lie past the restored step).
   121	    # Both matter, and they enter in different places. The optimizer heuristic scales learning rate,
   122	    # adam_lr, and epsilon from a token budget (`num_train_steps * batch * seq`), which fixes the
   123	    # peak. Warmup and decay are *fractions* of `TrainerConfig.num_train_steps`, so that field has to
   124	    # carry the schedule length too -- passing `num_steps` there warms up in `0.01 * num_steps` and
   125	    # decays to `min_lr_ratio` by the end of the short run, which is a whole miniature schedule
   126	    # rather than the head of a long one. Default keeps the two equal, which is the previous behavior.
   127	    if schedule_steps is not None and schedule_steps <= 0:
   128	        raise ValueError(f"schedule_steps must be positive, got {schedule_steps}")
   129	    if schedule_steps is not None and schedule_steps < num_steps:
   130	        raise ValueError(f"schedule_steps={schedule_steps} must be at least num_steps={num_steps}")
   131	    total_schedule_steps = schedule_steps if schedule_steps is not None else num_steps
   132	    _, optimizer = build_hero_configs(
   133	        num_train_steps=total_schedule_steps,
   134	        batch_size=batch_size,
   135	    )

   137	        raise ValueError(f"remat_save must be one of {sorted(REMAT_SAVE_BUNDLES)}, got {remat_save!r}")
   138	    model = HERO_MODEL_CONFIG
   139	    overrides = {
   140	        name: value
   141	        for name, value in (
   142	            ("num_experts", num_experts),
   143	            ("num_experts_per_token", num_experts_per_token),
   144	            ("intermediate_dim", intermediate_dim),
   145	            ("capacity_factor", capacity_factor),

   147	            ("moe_implementation", moe_implementation),

   149	        )
   150	        if value is not None
   151	    }
   152	    if overrides:

   552	    @named_call
   553	    def __call__(
   554	        self,
   555	        x: Float[Array, "B S D"],
   556	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array,
   557	        disable_rope: bool | jax.Array = False,
   558	        is_global: bool | jax.Array = False,
   559	    ) -> Float[Array, "B S D"]:
   560	        head_dim = self.cfg.inferred_head_dim
   561	        seq_len = x.shape[1]
   562	        batch_spec = _batch_spec()
   563	
   564	        q_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_q)
   565	        k_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_k)
   566	        v_flat = jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", x, self.w_v)
   567	        # SConv: depthwise causal conv after the K projection. segment_ids (packed-document
   568	        # boundaries) come from the mask so the conv never mixes across a document boundary.
   569	        _seg = mask.segment_ids if isinstance(mask, AttentionMask) else None
   570	        sconv_segment_ids = _seg[0] if _seg is not None else None
   571	        if self.sconv_k is not None:
   572	            k_flat = self.sconv_k(k_flat, sconv_segment_ids)
   573	        q = rearrange(q_flat, "... (n d) -> ... n d", d=head_dim)
   574	        k = rearrange(k_flat, "... (m d) -> ... m d", d=head_dim)
   575	        v = rearrange(v_flat, "... (m d) -> ... m d", d=head_dim)
   576	
   577	        if self.cfg.local_kv_heads is not None and self.cfg.global_kv_heads is not None:
   578	            stored_kv_heads = self.cfg.stored_kv_heads
   579	
   580	            # Both branches must agree on sharding, not just shape: `lax.cond` compares full types
   581	            # under explicit mesh axes. The pass-through case keeps the projection's `model`-sharded
   582	            # head axis, while the align case slices to one head -- which cannot stay sharded -- and
   583	            # broadcasts back, giving an identical shape with a different sharding. Reshard both to
   584	            # the same spec. This is a no-op wherever the mesh leaves `model` at one, which is why
   585	            # the hero shape never hit it despite also setting local_kv_heads != global_kv_heads.
   586	            #
   587	            # Replicate the head axis rather than pinning it to `model`: a shape can carry fewer
   588	            # KV heads than the model axis is wide (d768 stores one), and the KV tensors are small
   589	            # enough -- at most a dozen heads of 128 -- that replication is not worth a special case.
   590	            kv_spec = P(_BATCH_AXES, None, None, None)
   591	
   592	            def _logical_kv(projection: jax.Array, num_kv_heads: int) -> jax.Array:
   593	                # Replicate before slicing, not after: narrowing a `model`-sharded head axis to a
   594	                # count that does not divide the mesh axis is unsupported.
   595	                projection = reshard(projection, kv_spec)
   596	                if num_kv_heads == stored_kv_heads:
   597	                    return projection
   598	                return align_kv_heads(projection[:, :, :num_kv_heads, :], num_q_heads=stored_kv_heads)
   599	
   600	            k, v = jax.lax.cond(
   601	                jnp.asarray(is_global, dtype=jnp.bool_),
   602	                lambda kv: (
   603	                    _logical_kv(kv[0], self.cfg.global_kv_heads),
   604	                    _logical_kv(kv[1], self.cfg.global_kv_heads),
   605	                ),
   606	                lambda kv: (
   607	                    _logical_kv(kv[0], self.cfg.local_kv_heads),
   608	                    _logical_kv(kv[1], self.cfg.local_kv_heads),
   609	                ),
   610	                (k, v),
   611	            )
   612	
   613	        q = rms_norm(q)
   614	        k = rms_norm(k)
   615	
   616	        # Half-RoPE: apply rotary embedding only to the first half of Q/K head_dim (second half is
   617	        # rope-free on every layer). ``disable_rope`` skips RoPE entirely on this layer -- long/global
   618	        # layers run rope-free. It rides in as a traced per-layer scalar from the layer scan, so RoPE
   619	        # is always computed and selected with ``jnp.where`` rather than a per-layer ``lax.cond``.
   620	        if self.cfg.rope_fused:
   621	            q, k = _apply_rotary_embedding_fused(
   622	                q,
   623	                k,
   624	                seq_len=seq_len,
   625	                head_dim=head_dim,
   626	                rotary_dim=head_dim // 2,
   627	                rope=self.cfg.rope,
   628	                disable_rope=disable_rope,
   629	            )
   630	        else:
   631	
   632	            def _rope(qh: jax.Array, kh: jax.Array) -> tuple[jax.Array, jax.Array]:
   633	                half = head_dim // 2
   634	                q_rot, k_rot = apply_rotary_embedding(
   635	                    qh[..., :half], kh[..., :half], seq_len=seq_len, head_dim=half, rope=self.cfg.rope
   636	                )
   637	                return (
   638	                    jnp.concatenate([q_rot, qh[..., half:]], axis=-1),
   639	                    jnp.concatenate([k_rot, kh[..., half:]], axis=-1),
   640	                )
   641	
   642	            if isinstance(disable_rope, bool):
   643	                if not disable_rope:
   644	                    q, k = _rope(q, k)
   645	            else:
   646	                q_roped, k_roped = _rope(q, k)
   647	                keep = ~jnp.asarray(disable_rope, dtype=jnp.bool_)
   648	                q = jnp.where(keep, q_roped, q)
   649	                k = jnp.where(keep, k_roped, k)
   650	        q = q * self.cfg.qk_mult
   651	        attn_out = attention(q, k, v, mask, implementation=self.cfg.attention_implementation)
   652	        # Exclusive Self Attention (XSA): subtract the component of yᵢ parallel to vᵢ, per head.
   653	        # zᵢ = yᵢ - (yᵢᵀvᵢ / ‖vᵢ‖²) vᵢ.
   654	        aligned_v = align_kv_heads(v, num_q_heads=attn_out.shape[2])
   655	        # GPU XSA with GQA can give attn_out a backend-specific head sharding;
   656	        # match v to that dynamic sharding before the per-head projection math.
   657	        aligned_v = reshard(aligned_v, _partition_spec_of(attn_out) or P(_BATCH_AXES, None, None, "model"))
   658	        dot = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.sum(attn_out * aligned_v, axis=-1, keepdims=True), XSA_MOMENT_REMAT_NAME)
   659	        v_norm_sq = tree_checkpoint_name(jnp.sum(aligned_v * aligned_v, axis=-1, keepdims=True), XSA_MOMENT_REMAT_NAME)
   660	        attn_out = attn_out - (dot / (v_norm_sq + 1e-6)) * aligned_v
   661	        # Headwise gating: sigmoid(x @ attn_gate) produces one scalar per head.
   662	        gate = 2 * jax.nn.sigmoid(jnp.einsum("bsd,dn->bsn", x, self.attn_gate))[..., None]
   663	        attn_out = gate * attn_out
   664	        # Merge heads into hidden dim while keeping model-axis sharding for w_o.
   665	        attn_out = jnp.reshape(
   666	            attn_out,
   667	            (*attn_out.shape[:-2], attn_out.shape[-2] * attn_out.shape[-1]),
   668	            out_sharding=P(_BATCH_AXES, None, "model"),
   669	        )
   670	        return jnp.einsum("bsh,hd->bsd", attn_out, self.w_o, out_sharding=batch_spec)

  1205	    @named_call
  1206	    def __call__(
  1207	        self,
  1208	        x: Float[Array, "B S D"],
  1209	        mask: AttentionMask | jax.Array,
  1210	        disable_rope: bool | jax.Array = False,
  1211	        is_global: bool | jax.Array = False,
  1212	    ) -> tuple[Float[Array, "B S D"], dict[str, jax.Array]]:
  1213	        # A remat policy acts on named intermediates, and a block argument is not one. This
  1214	        # reassignment routes every use below through the name, which lets the policy offload it.
  1215	        x = tree_checkpoint_name(x, LAYER_CARRY_REMAT_NAME)
  1216	        # segment_ids (packed-document boundaries) for the branch-output SConvs; None when unpacked.
  1217	        _seg = mask.segment_ids if isinstance(mask, AttentionMask) else None
  1218	        sconv_segment_ids = _seg[0] if _seg is not None else None
  1219	
  1220	        attn_in = self.attn_gated_norm(self.rms_attn(x))
  1221	        attn_out = self.attn(attn_in, mask, disable_rope=disable_rope, is_global=is_global)
  1222	        if self.sconv_attn is not None:
  1223	            attn_out = self.sconv_attn(attn_out, sconv_segment_ids)
  1224	        x = x + attn_out
  1225	        mlp_in = self.mlp_gated_norm(self.rms_mlp(x))
  1226	        mlp_out, router_stats = self.mlp(mlp_in)
  1227	        if self.shared is not None:
  1228	            for shared_expert in self.shared:
  1229	                mlp_out = mlp_out + shared_expert(mlp_in, activation=ActivationFunctionEnum.silu)
  1230	        if self.sconv_mlp is not None:
  1231	            mlp_out = self.sconv_mlp(mlp_out, sconv_segment_ids)
  1232	        x = x + mlp_out
  1233	        return x, router_stats
  1234	
  1235	
  1236	def _long_layer_schedule(num_layers: int, global_every: int) -> jax.Array:
  1237	    # Every global_every-th layer is full-causal, and the last layer always is, so a depth that is
  1238	    # not a multiple of global_every still ends on a global-context layer.
  1239	    layer_indices = jnp.arange(num_layers)
  1240	    return (((layer_indices + 1) % global_every) == 0) | (layer_indices == num_layers - 1)

  1249	    final_norm: RMSNorm
  1250	    final_gated_norm: GatedNorm

import jax, jax.numpy as jnp
from jax.ad_checkpoint import checkpoint_name

def f(x):
  v = checkpoint_name(jnp.mean(jnp.square(x), axis=-1, keepdims=True), 'variance')
  s = checkpoint_name(jax.lax.rsqrt(v + 1e-6), 'scale')
  return jnp.sum(x * s)
for name in ('variance','scale'):
  policy = jax.checkpoint_policies.save_only_these_names(name)
  g = jax.grad(jax.checkpoint(f, policy=policy))
  j = str(jax.make_jaxpr(g)(jnp.ones((4,8), jnp.float32)))
  print('\\nSAVE',name)
  for line in j.splitlines():
    if any(k in line for k in ('reduce_sum','integer_pow','rsqrt','remat2','name[')):
      print(line)

codex
The first lowering result is strong for the unsaved case: default and “all new names stripped” produce exactly the same 1,666,850-byte CUDA HLO, with the same SHA-256 and identical ragged-all-to-all operands. That makes a name-induced CSE/copy-insertion regression impossible in the default path because the GPU compiler receives the same module. I’m also applying the profiling workflow now to audit whether the 180–230 ms claim is actually supported by the measured kernel families, not merely plausible from the source.
